<p><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Emblem_of_India.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Emblem_of_India.svg/40px-Emblem_of_India.svg.png" decoding="async" width="40" height="64" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Emblem_of_India.svg/60px-Emblem_of_India.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Emblem_of_India.svg/80px-Emblem_of_India.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="549" data-file-height="875" /></a></span>
</p>
<a href="https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Category:_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" class="extiw" title="commons:Category: Atal Bihari Vajpayee"><b>Gallery</b>: Picture, Sound, Video</a></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_series" title="Template:Atal Bihari Vajpayee series"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_series" title="Template talk:Atal Bihari Vajpayee series"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_series&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Atal Bihari Vajpayee</b> (<small>Hindustani pronunciation:&#160;</small><span class="IPA nowrap" lang="und-Latn-fonipa" title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Hindi_and_Urdu" title="Help:IPA/Hindi and Urdu">[əʈəl<span class="wrap"> </span>bɪɦaːɾiː<span class="wrap"> </span>ʋaːdʒpai]</a></span>; 25&#160;December 1924&#160;– 16&#160;August 2018) was an Indian politician and poet who served three terms as the 10th <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">Prime Minister of India</a>, first for a term of 13&#160;days in 1996, then for a period of 13&#160;months from 1998 to 1999, followed by a full term from <a href="/wiki/1999_Indian_general_election" title="1999 Indian general election">1999</a> to <a href="/wiki/2004_Indian_general_election" title="2004 Indian general election">2004</a>. Vajpayee was one of the co-founders and a senior leader of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">BJP</a>. He was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Rashtriya_Swayamsevak_Sangh" title="Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh">RSS</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Hindutva" title="Hindutva">Hindu nationalist</a> volunteer organisation. He was the first Indian prime minister not of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">INC</a> to serve a full term in office. He was also a renowned <a href="/wiki/Poet" title="Poet">poet</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Writer" title="Writer">writer</a>.
</p><p>He was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_India" title="Parliament of India">Indian Parliament</a> for over five decades, having been elected ten times to the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a>, the lower house, and twice to the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>, the upper house. He served as the <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament,_Lok_Sabha" title="Member of Parliament, Lok Sabha">MP (LS)</a> for <a href="/wiki/Lucknow" title="Lucknow">Lucknow</a>, retiring from active politics in 2009 due to health concerns. He was among the founding members of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Jana_Sangh" title="Bharatiya Jana Sangh">BJS</a>, of which he was president from 1968 to 1972. The BJS merged with several other parties to form the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">JP</a>, which won the <a href="/wiki/1977_Indian_general_election" title="1977 Indian general election">1977 general election</a>. In March 1977, Vajpayee became the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_External_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of External Affairs (India)">Minister of External Affairs</a> in the cabinet of Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a>. He resigned in 1979, and the Janata alliance collapsed soon after. Former members of the BJS formed the BJP in 1980, with Vajpayee its first president.
</p><p>During <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Premiership of Atal Bihari Vajpayee">his tenure as prime minister</a>, India carried out the <a href="/wiki/Pokhran-II" title="Pokhran-II">Pokhran-II</a> nuclear tests in 1998. Vajpayee sought to improve <a href="/wiki/India%E2%80%93Pakistan_relations" title="India–Pakistan relations">diplomatic relations with Pakistan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lahore_Declaration" title="Lahore Declaration">travelling to Lahore</a> by bus to meet with Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Nawaz_Sharif" title="Nawaz Sharif">Nawaz Sharif</a>. After the <a href="/wiki/Kargil_War" title="Kargil War">1999 Kargil War</a> with Pakistan, he sought to restore relations through engagement with President <a href="/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf" title="Pervez Musharraf">Pervez Musharraf</a>, inviting him to India for a summit at <a href="/wiki/Agra" title="Agra">Agra</a>. Vajpayee's government introduced many <a href="/wiki/Economic_development_in_India" title="Economic development in India">domestic economic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Infrastructure-based_development" title="Infrastructure-based development">infrastructural reforms</a>, including encouraging the <a href="/wiki/Private_sector" title="Private sector">private sector</a> and <a href="/wiki/Foreign_investments" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign investments">foreign investments</a>, reducing <a href="/wiki/Government_waste" class="mw-redirect" title="Government waste">governmental waste</a>, encouraging <a href="/wiki/Research_and_development" title="Research and development">research and development</a> and <a href="/wiki/Privatisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Privatisation">privatisation</a> of some government owned corporations.<sup id="cite_ref-auto5_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto5-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> During his reign, India's security was threatened by a number of violent incidents including <a href="/wiki/2001_Indian_Parliament_attack" title="2001 Indian Parliament attack">2001 Indian Parliament attack</a> and <a href="/wiki/2002_Gujarat_riots" title="2002 Gujarat riots">2002 Gujarat riots</a> which ultimately caused his defeat in <a href="/wiki/2004_Indian_general_election" title="2004 Indian general election">2004 general election</a>.
</p><p>The administration of <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a> declared in 2014 that Vajpayee's birthday, 25&#160;December, would be marked as <a href="/wiki/Good_Governance_Day" title="Good Governance Day">Good Governance Day</a>. In 2015, he was conferred India's highest civilian honour, the <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Ratna" title="Bharat Ratna">Bharat Ratna</a>, by the <a href="/wiki/President_of_India" title="President of India">president of India</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pranab_Mukherjee" title="Pranab Mukherjee">Pranab Mukherjee</a>. He died in 2018 of age-related illness.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life_and_education">Early life and education</span></h2>
<p>Vajpayee was born into a <a href="/wiki/Hindu" class="mw-redirect" title="Hindu">Hindu</a> <a href="/wiki/Brahmin" title="Brahmin">Brahmin</a> family on 25&#160;December 1924 in <a href="/wiki/Gwalior" title="Gwalior">Gwalior</a>, Madhya Pradesh.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> His mother was Krishna Devi and his father was Krishna Bihari Vajpayee.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEN_P2018_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEN_P2018-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> His father was a school teacher in their home town.<sup id="cite_ref-nytobit_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nytobit-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> His grandfather, Shyam Lal Vajpayee, had migrated to <a href="/wiki/Morena" title="Morena">Morena</a> near Gwalior from his ancestral village of <a href="/wiki/Bateshwar,_Uttar_Pradesh" title="Bateshwar, Uttar Pradesh">Bateshwar</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Agra_district" title="Agra district">Agra district</a> of <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEN_P2018_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEN_P2018-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vajpayee did his schooling at the Saraswati Shishu Mandir in Gwalior. In 1934, he was admitted to the Anglo-Vernacular Middle (AVM) School in <a href="/wiki/Badnagar" title="Badnagar">Barnagar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ujjain_district" title="Ujjain district">Ujjain district</a>, after his father joined as headmaster. He subsequently attended Gwalior's Victoria College, <a href="/wiki/Agra_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Agra University">Agra University</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Maharani_Laxmi_Bai_Govt._College_of_Excellence" title="Maharani Laxmi Bai Govt. College of Excellence">Maharani Laxmi Bai Govt. College of Excellence</a>) where he graduated with a Bachelor of Arts in <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a>, <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sanskrit" title="Sanskrit">Sanskrit</a>. He completed his post-graduation with a Master of Arts in <a href="/wiki/Political_science" title="Political science">political science</a> from <a href="/wiki/DAV_College,_Kanpur" title="DAV College, Kanpur">DAV College, Kanpur</a>, <a href="/wiki/Agra_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Agra University">Agra University</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:1_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_works_as_activist">Early works as activist</span></h2>
<p>His activism started in Gwalior with Arya Kumar Sabha, the youth wing of the <a href="/wiki/Arya_Samaj" title="Arya Samaj">Arya Samaj</a> movement, of which he became the general secretary in 1944. He also joined the <a href="/wiki/Rashtriya_Swayamsevak_Sangh" title="Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh">Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh</a> (RSS) in 1939 as a <i>swayamsevak</i>, or volunteer. Influenced by <a href="/wiki/Babasaheb_Apte" title="Babasaheb Apte">Babasaheb Apte</a>, he attended the Officers Training Camp of the RSS during 1940 to 1944, becoming a <i>pracharak</i> (RSS terminology for a full-time worker) in 1947. He gave up studying law due to the <a href="/wiki/Partition_of_India" title="Partition of India">partition</a> riots. He was sent to Uttar Pradesh as a <i>vistarak</i> (a probationary <i>pracharak</i>) and soon began working for the newspapers of <a href="/wiki/Deendayal_Upadhyaya" title="Deendayal Upadhyaya">Deendayal Upadhyaya</a>: <i>Rashtradharma</i> (a Hindi monthly), <i><a href="/wiki/Panchjanya_(magazine)" title="Panchjanya (magazine)">Panchjanya</a></i> (a Hindi weekly), and the dailies <i>Swadesh</i> and <i>Veer Arjun</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJaffrelot1996131–132_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJaffrelot1996131–132-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By 1942, at the age of 16 years, Vajpayee became an active member of the <a href="/wiki/Rashtriya_Swayamsevak_Sangh" title="Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh">Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh</a> (RSS). Although the RSS had chosen not to participate in the <a href="/wiki/Quit_India_Movement" title="Quit India Movement">Quit India Movement</a>, in August 1942, Vajpayee and his elder brother Prem were arrested for 24 days during the <a href="/wiki/Quit_India_Movement" title="Quit India Movement">Quit India Movement</a>. He was released after giving a written statement that while he was a part of the crowd, he did not participate in the militant events in Bateshwar on 27 August 1942. Throughout his life, including after he became prime minister, Vajpayee has labelled the allegation of participation in the Quit India Movement to be a false rumour.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Early_political_career_.281947.E2.80.931975.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_political_career_(1947–1975)">Early political career (1947–1975)</span></h2>
<p>In 1951, Vajpayee was seconded by the RSS, along with <a href="/wiki/Deendayal_Upadhyaya" title="Deendayal Upadhyaya">Deendayal Upadhyaya</a>, to work for the newly formed <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Jana_Sangh" title="Bharatiya Jana Sangh">Bharatiya Jana Sangh</a>, a Hindu right-wing political party associated with the RSS. He was appointed as a national secretary of the party in charge of the Northern region, based in Delhi. He soon became a follower and aide of party leader <a href="/wiki/Syama_Prasad_Mukherjee" title="Syama Prasad Mukherjee">Syama Prasad Mukherjee</a>. In the <a href="/wiki/1957_Indian_general_election" title="1957 Indian general election">1957 Indian general election</a>, Vajpayee contested elections to the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a>, the lower house of the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian Parliament">Indian Parliament</a>. He lost to <a href="/wiki/Mahendra_Pratap" class="mw-redirect" title="Mahendra Pratap">Raja Mahendra Pratap</a> in <a href="/wiki/Mathura" title="Mathura">Mathura</a>, but was elected from <a href="/wiki/Balrampur" title="Balrampur">Balrampur</a>. In the Lok Sabha his oratorial skills so impressed Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a> that he predicted that Vajpayee would someday become the prime minister of India.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vajpayee's oratorial skills won him the reputation of being the most eloquent defender of the Jana Sangh's policies.<sup id="cite_ref-Guha_Scroll_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guha_Scroll-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> After the death of <a href="/wiki/Deendayal_Upadhyaya" title="Deendayal Upadhyaya">Deendayal Upadhyaya</a>, the leadership of the Jana Sangh passed to Vajpayee.<sup id="cite_ref-Scroll_14-06-29_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scroll_14-06-29-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> He became the national president of the Jana Sangh in 1968,<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> running the party along with <a href="/wiki/Nanaji_Deshmukh" title="Nanaji Deshmukh">Nanaji Deshmukh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Balraj_Madhok" title="Balraj Madhok">Balraj Madhok</a>, and <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">L. K. Advani</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Scroll_14-06-29_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scroll_14-06-29-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Janata_Party_and_the_BJP_.281975.E2.80.931995.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Janata_Party_and_the_BJP_(1975–1995)">Janata Party and the BJP (1975–1995)</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg/220px-Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="260" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg/330px-Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg/440px-Atlaji_addressing_political_rally_with_kaushal_ji_.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1845" data-file-height="2184" /></a><figcaption>Vajpayee addressing a political rally in 1977.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Vajpayee was arrested along with several other opposition leaders during the <a href="/wiki/The_Emergency_(India)" title="The Emergency (India)">Internal Emergency</a> imposed by Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> in 1975.<sup id="cite_ref-nytobit_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nytobit-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECoomi_Kapoor201646_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECoomi_Kapoor201646-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Initially interned in <a href="/wiki/Bangalore" title="Bangalore">Bangalore</a>, Vajpayee appealed his imprisonment on the grounds of bad health, and was moved to a hospital in Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-Nag_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nag-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In December 1976, Vajpayee ordered the student activists of the <a href="/wiki/Akhil_Bharatiya_Vidyarthi_Parishad" title="Akhil Bharatiya Vidyarthi Parishad">ABVP</a> to tender an unconditional apology to <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> for perpetrating violence and disorder.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The ABVP student leaders refused to obey his order.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gandhi ended the state of emergency in 1977. A coalition of parties, including the BJS, came together to form the <a href="/wiki/Janata_Party" title="Janata Party">Janata Party</a>, which won the <a href="/wiki/1977_Indian_general_election" title="1977 Indian general election">1977 general elections</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC1_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC1-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a>, the chosen leader of the alliance, became the prime minister. Vajpayee served as the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_External_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of External Affairs (India)">minister of external affairs</a>, or foreign minister, in Desai's cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-IE_18-08-16_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IE_18-08-16-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> As foreign minister, Vajpayee became the first person in 1977 to deliver a speech to the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a> in <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-IE_18-08-16_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IE_18-08-16-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi,_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi%2C_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg/220px-Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi%2C_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi%2C_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg/330px-Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi%2C_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi%2C_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg/440px-Officials_of_India_welcome_Jimmy_Carter_and_Rosalynn_Carter_during_an_arrival_ceremony_in_New_Delhi%2C_India_-_NARA_-_177371.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2958" data-file-height="1995" /></a><figcaption>Foreign Minister Vajpayee (far right) and Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a> (third from right, front row) with US President <a href="/wiki/Jimmy_Carter" title="Jimmy Carter">Jimmy Carter</a> during his 1978 visit to India.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1979, Desai and Vajpayee resigned, triggering the collapse of the Janata Party.<sup id="cite_ref-Nag_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nag-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The erstwhile members of the Bharatiya Jana Sangh came together to form the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> (BJP) in 1980, with Vajpayee as its first President.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Leading up to <a href="/wiki/Operation_Blue_Star" title="Operation Blue Star">Operation Bluestar</a>, there were several protests by <a href="/wiki/Sangh_Parivar" title="Sangh Parivar">Sangh Parivar</a>, including a march led by <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">LK Advani</a> and Vajpayee of the Bhartiya Janta Party to protest against the lack of government action and to demand that the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Army" title="Indian Army">Indian Army</a> be sent into the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Temple" title="Golden Temple">Golden Temple</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1984 general elections were held in the wake of Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi%27s_assassination" class="mw-redirect" title="Indira Gandhi&#39;s assassination">Indira Gandhi's assassination</a> by her Sikh bodyguards. While he had won the 1977 and the 1980 elections from New Delhi, Vajpayee shifted to his home town Gwalior for the election.<sup id="cite_ref-Mint_Elections_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mint_Elections-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Vidya Razdan was initially tipped to be the Congress (I) candidate. Instead, <a href="/wiki/Madhavrao_Scindia" title="Madhavrao Scindia">Madhavrao Scindia</a>, scion of the <a href="/wiki/Scindia" class="mw-redirect" title="Scindia">Gwalior royal family</a>, was brought in on the last day of filing nominations.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee lost to Scindia, managing to secure only 29% of the votes.<sup id="cite_ref-Mint_Elections_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mint_Elections-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Under Vajpayee, the BJP moderated the Hindu-nationalist position of the Jana Sangh, emphasising its connection to the Janata Party and expressing support for <a href="/wiki/Gandhian_Socialism" class="mw-redirect" title="Gandhian Socialism">Gandhian Socialism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Malik_and_Singh_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Malik_and_Singh-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The ideological shift did not bring it success: Indira Gandhi's assassination generated sympathy for the Congress, leading to a massive victory at the polls. The BJP won only two seats in parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-Malik_and_Singh_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Malik_and_Singh-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee offered to quit as party president following BJP's dismal performance in the election,<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> but stayed in the post until 1986.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> He was elected to the Rajya Sabha in 1986 from Madhya Pradesh,<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> and was briefly the leader of the BJP in Parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1986, <a href="/wiki/L._K._Advani" title="L. K. Advani">L. K. Advani</a> took office as president of the BJP.<sup id="cite_ref-IndiaToday_Elections_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IndiaToday_Elections-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Under him, the BJP returned to a policy of hardline Hindu nationalism.<sup id="cite_ref-Malik_and_Singh_30-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Malik_and_Singh-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> It became the political voice of the <a href="/wiki/Ram_Janmabhoomi" title="Ram Janmabhoomi">Ram Janmabhoomi</a> Mandir Movement, which sought to build a temple dedicated to the Hindu deity <a href="/wiki/Rama" title="Rama">Rama</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya" title="Ayodhya">Ayodhya</a>. The temple would be built at a site believed to be the birthplace of Rama after demolishing a 16th-century mosque, called the <a href="/wiki/Babri_Masjid" title="Babri Masjid">Babri Masjid</a>, which then stood there.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The strategy paid off for the BJP; it won 86 seats in the Lok Sabha in the <a href="/wiki/1989_Indian_general_election" title="1989 Indian general election">1989 general election</a>, making its support crucial to the government of <a href="/wiki/V._P._Singh" title="V. P. Singh">V. P. Singh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Malik_and_Singh_30-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Malik_and_Singh-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> In December 1992, a group of religious volunteers led by members of the BJP, the Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh (RSS) and the Vishwa Hindu Parishad (VHP), tore down the mosque.<sup id="cite_ref-Caravan_Babri_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Caravan_Babri-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Guha_Scroll_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guha_Scroll-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He served as Member of Parliament, Lok Sabha, for various terms starting at Balrampur from 1957–1962. He served again from Balrampur from 1967–1971, then from Gwalior from 1971–1977, and then from New Delhi from 1977–1984. Finally, he served from Lucknow from 1991–2009.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Terms_as_prime_minister_.281996.2C_1998-_99_and_1999.E2.80.932004.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Terms_as_prime_minister_(1996,_1998-_99_and_1999–2004)">Terms as prime minister (1996, 1998- 99 and 1999–2004)</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Premiership of Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Premiership of Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="First_term:_May_1996">First term: May 1996</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/First_Vajpayee_ministry" title="First Vajpayee ministry">First Vajpayee ministry</a></div>
<p>During a BJP conference in Mumbai in November 1995, BJP President Advani declared that Vajpayee would be the party's prime ministerial candidate in the forthcoming elections. Vajpayee himself was reported to be unhappy with the announcement, responding by saying that the party needed to win the election first.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The BJP became the single largest party in Parliament in the <a href="/wiki/1996_Indian_general_election" title="1996 Indian general election">1996 general election</a>, helped by religious polarisation across the country as a result of the demolition of the Babri Masjid.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha2007633_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2007633-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEM._L._Ahuja1998208_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEM._L._Ahuja1998208-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Indian president <a href="/wiki/Shankar_Dayal_Sharma" title="Shankar Dayal Sharma">Shankar Dayal Sharma</a> invited Vajpayee to form the government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMuller2012628_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMuller2012628-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee was sworn in as the 10th prime minister of India,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChitkaraŚarmā1997268_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChitkaraŚarmā1997268-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> but the BJP failed to muster a majority among members of the Lok Sabha. Vajpayee resigned after 16 days, when it became clear that he did not have enough support to form a government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChitkaraŚarmā1997268_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChitkaraŚarmā1997268-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESumantra_Bose201379_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESumantra_Bose201379-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> In this short period, he also created and administered the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Consumer_Affairs,_Food_and_Public_Distribution" title="Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution">Ministry of Consumer Affairs, Food and Public Distribution</a>.
</p>
<h3><span id="Second_term:_1998.E2.80.931999"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Second_term:_1998–1999">Second term: 1998–1999</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Second_Vajpayee_ministry" title="Second Vajpayee ministry">Second Vajpayee ministry</a></div>
<p>After the fall of the two <a href="/wiki/Third_Front_(India)" title="Third Front (India)">United Front</a> governments between 1996 and 1998, the Lok Sabha was dissolved and fresh elections were held. The <a href="/wiki/1998_Indian_general_election" title="1998 Indian general election">1998 general elections</a> again put the BJP ahead of others. A number of political parties joined the BJP to form the <a href="/wiki/National_Democratic_Alliance_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="National Democratic Alliance (India)">National Democratic Alliance</a> (NDA), and Vajpayee was sworn in as the prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> The coalition was an uneasy one,<sup id="cite_ref-BBC1_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC1-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> as apart from the <a href="/wiki/Shiv_Sena" title="Shiv Sena">Shiv Sena</a>, none of the other parties espoused the BJP's Hindu-nationalist ideology.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha2007662_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2007662-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee has been credited for managing this coalition successfully, while facing ideological pressure from the hardline wing of the party and from the RSS.<sup id="cite_ref-Nag_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nag-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee's government lasted 13 months until mid-1999 when the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Anna_Dravida_Munnetra_Kazhagam" title="All India Anna Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam">All India Anna Dravida Munnetra Kazhagam</a> (AIADMK) under <a href="/wiki/J._Jayalalithaa" title="J. Jayalalithaa">J. Jayalalithaa</a> withdrew its support.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The government lost the ensuing vote of confidence motion in the Lok Sabha by a single vote on 17 April 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETurner2016818_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETurner2016818-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> As the opposition was unable to come up with the numbers to form the new government, the Lok Sabha was again dissolved and fresh elections were held.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Nuclear_tests">Nuclear tests</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Pokhran-II" title="Pokhran-II">Pokhran-II</a></div>
<p>In May 1998, India conducted <a href="/wiki/Pokhran-II" title="Pokhran-II">five underground nuclear tests</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Pokhran" title="Pokhran">Pokhran</a> desert in <a href="/wiki/Rajasthan" title="Rajasthan">Rajasthan</a>, 24 years after its first nuclear test (<a href="/wiki/Smiling_Buddha" title="Smiling Buddha">Smiling Buddha</a>) in 1974. Two weeks later, Pakistan responded with its own nuclear tests making it the newest nation with declared nuclear capability.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha2007673–675_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2007673–675-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> While some nations, such as France, endorsed India's right to defensive nuclear power,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAjai_K._Rai2009162_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAjai_K._Rai2009162-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> others including the United States, Canada, Japan, Britain and the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> imposed sanctions on information, resources and technology to India. In spite of intense international criticism and steady decline in foreign investment and trade, the nuclear tests were popular domestically. In effect, the international sanctions imposed failed to sway India from weaponising its nuclear capability. US sanctions against India and Pakistan were eventually lifted after just six months.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Lahore_summit">Lahore summit</span></h4>
<p>In late 1998 and early 1999, Vajpayee began a push for a full-scale diplomatic peace process with Pakistan. With the historic inauguration of the Delhi-Lahore bus service in February 1999, Vajpayee initiated a new peace process aimed towards permanently resolving the Kashmir dispute and other conflicts with Pakistan. The resultant <a href="/wiki/Lahore_Declaration" title="Lahore Declaration">Lahore Declaration</a> espoused a commitment to dialogue, expanded trade relations and mutual friendship and envisaged a goal of denuclearised South Asia. This eased the tension created by the 1998 nuclear tests, not only within the two nations but also in South Asia and the rest of the world.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="AIADMK.27s_withdrawal_from_coalition"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="AIADMK's_withdrawal_from_coalition">AIADMK's withdrawal from coalition</span></h4>
<p>The AIADMK had continually threatened to withdraw from the coalition and national leaders repeatedly flew down from Delhi to Chennai to pacify the AIADMK general secretary <a href="/wiki/J._Jayalalithaa" title="J. Jayalalithaa">J. Jayalalithaa</a>. However, in May 1999, the AIADMK did pull the plug on the NDA, and the Vajpayee administration was reduced to a caretaker status pending <a href="/wiki/1999_Indian_general_election" title="1999 Indian general election">fresh elections scheduled for October 1999</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Kargil_War">Kargil War</span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg/220px-BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="176" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg/330px-BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg/440px-BSF_Role_in_Kargil_War-3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="489" data-file-height="392" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Vajpayee with Indian troops and other dignitaries at Kargil after the war in 1999</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Kargil_War" title="Kargil War">Kargil War</a></div>
<p>In May 1999 some Kashmiri shepherds discovered the presence of militants and non-uniformed Pakistani soldiers (many with official identifications and <a href="/wiki/Pakistan_Army" title="Pakistan Army">Pakistan Army</a>'s custom weaponry) in the Kashmir Valley, where they had taken control of border hilltops and unmanned border posts. The incursion was centred around the town of <a href="/wiki/Kargil" title="Kargil">Kargil</a>, but also included the <a href="/wiki/Batalik" title="Batalik">Batalik</a> and <a href="/wiki/Akhnoor" title="Akhnoor">Akhnoor</a> sectors and artillery exchanges at the <a href="/wiki/Siachen_Glacier" title="Siachen Glacier">Siachen Glacier</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha2007675–678_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2007675–678-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Indian army responded with Operation Vijay, which launched on 26 May 1999. This saw the Indian military fighting thousands of militants and soldiers in the midst of heavy artillery shelling and while facing extremely cold weather, snow and treacherous terrain at the high altitude.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMyra201727–66_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMyra201727–66-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Over 500 Indian soldiers were killed in the three-month-long Kargil War, and it is estimated around 600–4,000 Pakistani militants and soldiers died as well.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERodrigo2006_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERodrigo2006-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> India pushed back the Pakistani militants and <a href="/wiki/Northern_Light_Infantry" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Light Infantry">Northern Light Infantry</a> soldiers. Almost 70% of the territory was recaptured by India.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMyra201727–66_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMyra201727–66-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee sent a "secret letter" to U.S. President <a href="/wiki/Bill_Clinton" title="Bill Clinton">Bill Clinton</a> that if Pakistani infiltrators did not withdraw from the Indian territory, "we will get them out, one way or the other"&#160;- meaning he did not rule out crossing the <a href="/wiki/Line_of_Control" title="Line of Control">Line of Control</a> (LoC), or was the use of nuclear weapons.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After Pakistan suffered heavy losses, and with both the United States and China refusing to condone the incursion or threaten India to stop its military operations, General <a href="/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf" title="Pervez Musharraf">Pervez Musharraf</a> was <a href="/wiki/Recalcitrant" class="mw-redirect" title="Recalcitrant">recalcitrant</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nawaz_Sharif" title="Nawaz Sharif">Nawaz Sharif</a> asked the remaining militants to stop and withdraw to positions along the LoC.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The militants were not willing to accept orders from Sharif but the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Light_Infantry" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Light Infantry">NLI</a> soldiers withdrew.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The militants were killed by the Indian army or forced to withdraw in skirmishes which continued even after the announcement of withdrawal by Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_64-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Third_term:_1999.E2.80.932004"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Third_term:_1999–2004">Third term: 1999–2004</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Third_Vajpayee_ministry" title="Third Vajpayee ministry">Third Vajpayee ministry</a></div>
<h4><span id="1999.E2.80.932002"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="1999–2002">1999–2002</span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/1999_Indian_general_election" title="1999 Indian general election">1999 general elections</a> were held in the aftermath of the Kargil operations. The BJP-led NDA won 303 seats out of the 543 seats in the Lok Sabha, securing a comfortable and stable majority.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> On 13 October 1999, Vajpayee took oath as the prime minister of India for the third time.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A national crisis emerged in December 1999, when <a href="/wiki/Indian_Airlines_flight_814_hijacking" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian Airlines flight 814 hijacking">Indian Airlines flight IC 814</a> from <a href="/wiki/Kathmandu" title="Kathmandu">Kathmandu</a> to New Delhi was hijacked by five terrorists and flown to <a href="/wiki/Taliban" title="Taliban">Taliban</a>-ruled Afghanistan.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The hijackers made several demands including the release of certain terrorists like <a href="/wiki/Masood_Azhar" title="Masood Azhar">Masood Azhar</a> from prison. Under pressure, the government ultimately caved in. <a href="/wiki/Jaswant_Singh" title="Jaswant Singh">Jaswant Singh</a>, the minister of external affairs at the time, flew with the terrorists to Afghanistan and exchanged them for the passengers.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg/220px-Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg/330px-Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg/440px-Vladimir_Putin_6_November_2001-5.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="334" /></a><figcaption>Vajpayee with Russian president <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> on 6 November 2001</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:President_Bill_Clinton_and_Prime_Minister_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_shake_hands_after_signing_a_vision_statement,_Hyderabad_House,_New_Delhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/President_Bill_Clinton_and_Prime_Minister_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_shake_hands_after_signing_a_vision_statement%2C_Hyderabad_House%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/220px-President_Bill_Clinton_and_Prime_Minister_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_shake_hands_after_signing_a_vision_statement%2C_Hyderabad_House%2C_New_Delhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/President_Bill_Clinton_and_Prime_Minister_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_shake_hands_after_signing_a_vision_statement%2C_Hyderabad_House%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/330px-President_Bill_Clinton_and_Prime_Minister_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_shake_hands_after_signing_a_vision_statement%2C_Hyderabad_House%2C_New_Delhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/5c/President_Bill_Clinton_and_Prime_Minister_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_shake_hands_after_signing_a_vision_statement%2C_Hyderabad_House%2C_New_Delhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="398" data-file-height="265" /></a><figcaption>Vajpayee meeting U.S. president <a href="/wiki/Bill_Clinton" title="Bill Clinton">Bill Clinton</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad_House" title="Hyderabad House">Hyderabad House</a><br />on 21 March 2000</figcaption></figure>
<p>In March 2000, <a href="/wiki/Bill_Clinton" title="Bill Clinton">Bill Clinton</a>, the President of the United States, paid a state visit to India.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> This was the first state visit to India by a U.S. president in 22 years, since President Jimmy Carter's visit in 1978.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> President Clinton's visit was hailed as a significant milestone in relations between the two nations.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee and Clinton had wide-ranging discussions on bilateral, regional and international developments.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The visit led to expansion in trade and economic ties between India and the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-LATimes_Obit_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LATimes_Obit-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> A vision document on the future course of Indo-U.S. relations was signed during the visit.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Domestically, the BJP-led government was influenced by the RSS, but owing to its dependence on coalition support, it was impossible for the BJP to push items like building the Ram Janmabhoomi temple in Ayodhya, repealing <a href="/wiki/Article_370_of_the_Constitution_of_India" title="Article 370 of the Constitution of India">Article 370</a> which gave a special status to the state of Kashmir, or enacting a <a href="/wiki/Uniform_civil_code" class="mw-redirect" title="Uniform civil code">uniform civil code</a> applicable to adherents of all religions. On 17 January 2000, there were reports of the RSS and some BJP hard-liners threatening to restart the Jan Sangh, the precursor to the BJP, because of their discontent over Vajpayee's rule. Former president of the Jan Sangh Balraj Madhok had written a letter to the then-RSS chief <a href="/wiki/Rajendra_Singh_(RSS)" title="Rajendra Singh (RSS)">Rajendra Singh</a> for support.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The BJP was, however, accused of "saffronising" the official state education curriculum and apparatus, saffron being the colour of the RSS flag of the RSS, and a symbol of the Hindu nationalism movement.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Home Minister L. K. Advani and the Human Resource Development Minister (now called Education Minister)<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Murli_Manohar_Joshi" title="Murli Manohar Joshi">Murli Manohar Joshi</a> were indicted in the 1992 <a href="/wiki/Demolition_of_the_Babri_Masjid" title="Demolition of the Babri Masjid">Babri Mosque demolition</a> case for inciting a mob of activists. Vajpayee himself came under public scrutiny owing to his controversial speech one day prior to the mosque demolition.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>These years were accompanied by infighting in the administration and confusion regarding the direction of government.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee's weakening health was also a subject of public interest, and he underwent a major knee-replacement surgery at the <a href="/wiki/Breach_Candy_Hospital" title="Breach Candy Hospital">Breach Candy Hospital</a> in <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a> to relieve intense pressure upon his legs.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2001, the <a href="/wiki/Tehelka" title="Tehelka">Tehelka</a> group released a sting operation video named <a href="/wiki/Operation_West_End" title="Operation West End">Operation West End</a> which showed BJP president <a href="/wiki/Bangaru_Laxman" title="Bangaru Laxman">Bangaru Laxman</a>, senior army officers and NDA members accepting bribes from journalists posing as agents and businessmen.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The Defence Minister <a href="/wiki/George_Fernandes" title="George Fernandes">George Fernandes</a> was forced to resign following the <a href="/wiki/Barak_Missile_scandal" title="Barak Missile scandal">Barak Missile scandal</a> involving the botched supplies of coffins for the soldiers killed in Kargil, and the findings of an inquiry commission that the government could have prevented the Kargil invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vajpayee initiated talks with Pakistan, and invited Pakistani president Pervez Musharraf to <a href="/wiki/Agra" title="Agra">Agra</a> for a <a href="/wiki/Agra_summit" class="mw-redirect" title="Agra summit">joint summit</a>. President Musharraf was believed to be the principal architect of the Kargil War in India.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> By accepting him as the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Pakistan" title="President of Pakistan">President of Pakistan</a>, Vajpayee chose to move forward leaving behind the Kargil War. But after three days of much fanfare, which included Musharraf visiting his birthplace in Delhi, the summit failed to achieve a breakthrough as President Musharraf declined to leave aside the issue of Kashmir.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="2001_attack_on_Parliament">2001 attack on Parliament</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/2001_Indian_Parliament_attack" title="2001 Indian Parliament attack">2001 Indian Parliament attack</a></div>
<p>On 13 December 2001, a group of masked, armed men with fake IDs stormed <a href="/wiki/Parliament_House_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament House (India)">Parliament House</a> in Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The terrorists managed to kill several security guards, but the building was sealed off swiftly and security forces cornered and killed the men who were later proven to be Pakistan nationals.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJ._N._Dixit2003_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJ._N._Dixit2003-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee ordered Indian troops to mobilise for war, leading to an estimated 500,000<sup id="cite_ref-ParliamentAttackBrinkWar_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ParliamentAttackBrinkWar-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> to 750,000<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_2002_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_2002-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Indian soldiers positioned along the international border between India and Pakistan. Pakistan responded by mobilising its own troops along the border.<sup id="cite_ref-ParliamentAttackBrinkWar_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ParliamentAttackBrinkWar-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> A terrorist attack on an army garrison in Kashmir in May 2002 further escalated the situation. As the threat of war between two nuclear capable countries and the consequent possibility of a nuclear exchange loomed large, international diplomatic mediation focused on defusing the situation.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> In October 2002, both India and Pakistan announced that they would withdraw their troops from the border.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_2002_89-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_2002-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Vajpayee administration brought in the <a href="/wiki/Prevention_of_Terrorism_Act,_2002" title="Prevention of Terrorism Act, 2002">Prevention of Terrorism Act</a> in 2002. The act was aimed at curbing terrorist threats by strengthening powers of government authorities to investigate and act against suspects.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> It was passed in a joint session of the parliament, amidst concerns that the law would be misused.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Another political disaster hit his government between December 2001 and March 2002: the VHP held the Government hostage in a major standoff in <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya" title="Ayodhya">Ayodhya</a> over the <a href="/wiki/Ram_Janmabhoomi" title="Ram Janmabhoomi">Ram temple</a>. On the 10th anniversary of the destruction of the <a href="/wiki/Babri_Masjid" title="Babri Masjid">Babri mosque</a>, the VHP wanted to perform a <i>shila daan</i>, or a ceremony laying the foundation stone of the cherished temple at the disputed site.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Thousands of VHP activists amassed and threatened to overrun the site and forcibly perform the ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> A grave threat of not only communal violence, but an outright breakdown of law and order owing to the defiance of the government by a religious organisation hung over the nation. The incident, however, ended peacefully with a symbolic handover of a stone at a different location 1&#160;km away from the disputed site.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="2002_Gujarat_violence">2002 Gujarat violence</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/2002_Gujarat_riots" title="2002 Gujarat riots">2002 Gujarat riots</a></div>
<p>In February 2002, a train filled with Hindu pilgrims returning to Gujarat from <a href="/wiki/Ayodhya" title="Ayodhya">Ayodhya</a> stopped in the town of <a href="/wiki/Godhra" title="Godhra">Godhra</a>. A scuffle broke out between Hindu activists and Muslim residents, and the train was set on fire, leading to the deaths of 59 people. The charred bodies of the victims were displayed in public in the city of <a href="/wiki/Ahmedabad" title="Ahmedabad">Ahmedabad</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Vishwa_Hindu_Parishad" class="mw-redirect" title="Vishwa Hindu Parishad">Vishwa Hindu Parishad</a> called for a statewide strike in Gujarat. These decisions stoked anti-Muslim sentiments.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Blaming Muslims for the deaths, rampaging Hindu mobs killed thousands of Muslim men and women, destroying Muslim homes and places of worship. The violence raged for more than two months, and more than 1,000 people died.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT_Riots_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT_Riots-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> Gujarat was being ruled by a BJP government, with <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a> as the chief minister. The state government was criticised for mishandling the situation.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> It was accused of doing little to stop the violence, and even being complicit in encouraging it.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-NYT_Riots_99-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT_Riots-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vajpayee reportedly wanted to remove Modi, but was eventually prevailed upon by party members to not act against him.<sup id="cite_ref-Caravan_Modi_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Caravan_Modi-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> He travelled to Gujarat, visiting Godhra, and Ahmedabad, the site of the most violent riots. He announced financial aid for victims, and urged an end to the violence.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT_2002_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT_2002-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> While he condemned the violence,<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> he did not chastise Modi directly in public. When asked as to what would be his message to the chief minister in the event of the riots having taking place, Vajpayee responded that Modi must follow <i>raj dharma</i>, Hindi for ethical governance.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT_2002_104-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT_2002-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the meeting of the BJP national executive in Goa in April 2002, Vajpayee's speech generated controversy for its contents which included him saying: "Wherever Muslims live, they don't like to live in co-existence with others."<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister%27s_Office_(India)" title="Prime Minister&#39;s Office (India)">Prime Minister's Office</a> stated that these remarks had been taken out of context.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee was accused of doing nothing to stop the violence, and later admitted mistakes in handling the events.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/K._R._Narayanan" title="K. R. Narayanan">K. R. Narayanan</a>, then <a href="/wiki/President_of_India" title="President of India">president of India</a>, also blamed Vajpayee's government for failing to quell the violence.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDossani2008154_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDossani2008154-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> After the BJP's defeat in the 2004 general elections, Vajpayee admitted that not removing Modi had been a mistake.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="2002.E2.80.932004"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="2002–2004">2002–2004</span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_speaking_at_a_special_session_to_commorate_200th_session_of_Rajya_Sabha_in_the_Centre_Hall_of_Parliament_on_11_December,_2003.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_speaking_at_a_special_session_to_commorate_200th_session_of_Rajya_Sabha_in_the_Centre_Hall_of_Parliament_on_11_December%2C_2003.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_speaking_at_a_special_session_to_commorate_200th_session_of_Rajya_Sabha_in_the_Centre_Hall_of_Parliament_on_11_December%2C_2003.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_speaking_at_a_special_session_to_commorate_200th_session_of_Rajya_Sabha_in_the_Centre_Hall_of_Parliament_on_11_December%2C_2003.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="634" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Vajpayee speaking at a special session to commorate 200th session of <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a> in 2003.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In late 2002 and 2003 the government pushed through economic reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-ChinaDailyEconomy2004_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ChinaDailyEconomy2004-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> The country's GDP growth exceeded 7% every year from 2003 to 2007, following three years of sub-5% growth.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Increasing foreign investment,<sup id="cite_ref-ChinaDailyEconomy2004_112-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ChinaDailyEconomy2004-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> modernisation of public and industrial infrastructure, the creation of jobs, a rising high-tech and IT industry and urban modernisation and expansion improved the nation's international image. Good crop harvests and strong industrial expansion also helped the economy.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In May 2003, he announced before the parliament that he would make one last effort to achieve peace with Pakistan. The announcement ended a period of 16 months, following the 2001 attack on the Indian parliament, during which India had severed diplomatic ties with Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> Although diplomatic relations did not pick up immediately, visits were exchanged by high-level officials and the military standoff ended. The Pakistani President and Pakistani politicians, civil and religious leaders hailed this initiative as did the leaders of the United States, Europe and much of the world. In July 2003, Prime Minister Vajpayee visited China, and met with various Chinese leaders. He recognised <a href="/wiki/Tibet" title="Tibet">Tibet</a> as a part of China, which was welcomed by the Chinese leadership, and which, in the following year, recognised <a href="/wiki/Sikkim" title="Sikkim">Sikkim</a> as part of India. <a href="/wiki/China%E2%80%93India_relations" title="China–India relations">China–India relations</a> improved greatly in the following years.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Policies">Policies</span></h4>
<p>Vajpayee's government introduced many domestic economic and infrastructural reforms, including encouraging the private sector and foreign investments, reducing governmental waste, encouraging research and development and privatisation of some government owned corporations.<sup id="cite_ref-auto5_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto5-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Among Vajpayee's projects were the <a href="/wiki/National_Highways_Development_Project" title="National Highways Development Project">National Highways Development Project</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pradhan_Mantri_Gram_Sadak_Yojana" title="Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana">Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto4_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto4-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto1_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> In 2001, the Vajpayee government launched the <a href="/wiki/Sarva_Shiksha_Abhiyan" title="Sarva Shiksha Abhiyan">Sarva Shiksha Abhiyan</a> campaign, aimed at improving the quality of education in primary and secondary schools.<sup id="cite_ref-auto3_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto3-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto2_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="2004_general_election">2004 general election</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow,_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5,_2004.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow%2C_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5%2C_2004.jpg/220px-The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow%2C_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5%2C_2004.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="154" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow%2C_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5%2C_2004.jpg/330px-The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow%2C_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5%2C_2004.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow%2C_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5%2C_2004.jpg/440px-The_Prime_Minister_Shri_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee_casting_his_vote_at_a_polling_booth_in_Lucknow%2C_Uttar_Pradesh_on_May_5%2C_2004.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="632" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Vajpayee casting his vote at a polling booth in <a href="/wiki/Lucknow" title="Lucknow">Lucknow</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>, during the 2004 general election.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 2003, news reports suggested a tussle within the BJP with regard to sharing of leadership between Vajpayee and Deputy Prime Minister LK Advani.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> BJP president Venkaiah Naidu had suggested that Advani must lead the party politically at the 2004 general elections, referring to Vajpayee as <i>vikas purush</i>, Hindi for development man, and Advani as <i>loh purush</i>, iron man.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> When Vajpayee subsequently threatened retirement, Naidu backtracked, announcing that the party would contest the elections under the twin leadership of Vajpayee and Advani.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The NDA was widely expected to retain power after the <a href="/wiki/2004_Indian_general_election" title="2004 Indian general election">2004 general election</a>. It announced elections six months ahead of schedule, hoping to capitalise on economic growth, and Vajpayee's peace initiative with Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Guardian_2004_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_2004-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/13th_Lok_Sabha" title="13th Lok Sabha">13th Lok Sabha</a> was dissolved before the completion of its term. The BJP hoped to capitalise on a perceived 'feel-good factor' and BJP's recent successes in the Assembly elections in Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh and <a href="/wiki/Chhattisgarh" title="Chhattisgarh">Chhattisgarh</a>. Under the "<a href="/wiki/India_Shining" title="India Shining">India Shining</a>" campaign, it released ads proclaiming the economic growth of the nation under the government.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, the BJP could only win 138 seats in the 543-seat parliament,<sup id="cite_ref-Economist_2004_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economist_2004-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> with several prominent cabinet ministers being defeated.<sup id="cite_ref-Guardian_2004_126-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guardian_2004-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The NDA coalition won 185 seats. The Indian National Congress, led by <a href="/wiki/Sonia_Gandhi" title="Sonia Gandhi">Sonia Gandhi</a>, emerged as the single largest party, winning 145 seats in the election. The Congress and its allies, comprising many smaller parties, formed the <a href="/wiki/United_Progressive_Alliance" title="United Progressive Alliance">United Progressive Alliance</a>, accounting for 220 seats in the parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-Economist_2004_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economist_2004-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee resigned as prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The UPA, with the outside support of communist parties, formed the next government with <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> as the prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-premiership">Post-premiership</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Atal_Bihari_Bajpayee_and_the_BJP_President,_Shri_Rajnath_Singh_coming_out_from_the_polling_booth_after_casting_their_vote_for_the_Presidential_election_at_Parliament_House,_in_New_Delhi_on_July_19,_2007.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Atal_Bihari_Bajpayee_and_the_BJP_President%2C_Shri_Rajnath_Singh_coming_out_from_the_polling_booth_after_casting_their_vote_for_the_Presidential_election_at_Parliament_House%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_19%2C_2007.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="323" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Atal_Bihari_Bajpayee_and_the_BJP_President%2C_Shri_Rajnath_Singh_coming_out_from_the_polling_booth_after_casting_their_vote_for_the_Presidential_election_at_Parliament_House%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_19%2C_2007.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Atal_Bihari_Bajpayee_and_the_BJP_President%2C_Shri_Rajnath_Singh_coming_out_from_the_polling_booth_after_casting_their_vote_for_the_Presidential_election_at_Parliament_House%2C_in_New_Delhi_on_July_19%2C_2007.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1429" data-file-height="2100" /></a><figcaption>Vajpayee and <a href="/wiki/Rajnath_Singh" title="Rajnath Singh">Rajnath Singh</a> (left) during the voting for 2007 Indian Presidential election</figcaption></figure>
<p>In December 2005, Vajpayee announced his retirement from active politics, declaring that he would not contest in the next general election. In a famous statement at the BJP's silver jubilee rally at Mumbai's <a href="/wiki/Shivaji_Park" title="Shivaji Park">Shivaji Park</a>, Vajpayee announced that "Henceforth, <a href="/wiki/Lal_Krishna_Advani" class="mw-redirect" title="Lal Krishna Advani">Lal Krishna Advani</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pramod_Mahajan" title="Pramod Mahajan">Pramod Mahajan</a> will be the <a href="/wiki/Rama" title="Rama">Ram</a>-<a href="/wiki/Lakshmana" title="Lakshmana">Lakshman</a> [the two godly brothers much revered and worshipped by Hindus] of the BJP."<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vajpayee was referred to as the <i><a href="/wiki/Bhishma" title="Bhishma">Bhishma</a> Pitamah</i> of Indian politics by former prime minister <a href="/wiki/Manmohan_Singh" title="Manmohan Singh">Manmohan Singh</a> during a speech in the <a href="/wiki/Rajya_Sabha" title="Rajya Sabha">Rajya Sabha</a>, a reference to the character in the Hindu epic <a href="/wiki/Mahabharata" title="Mahabharata">Mahabharata</a> who was held in respect by two warring sides.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Vajpayee was hospitalised at <a href="/wiki/All_India_Institute_of_Medical_Sciences,_Delhi" class="mw-redirect" title="All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Delhi">All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Delhi</a> (AIIMS) for a chest infection and fever on 6 February 2009. He was put on ventilator support as his condition worsened but he eventually recuperated and was later discharged.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Unable to participate in the campaign for the <a href="/wiki/2009_Indian_general_election" title="2009 Indian general election">2009 general election</a> due to his poor health, he wrote a letter urging voters to back the BJP.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> His protege <a href="/wiki/Lalji_Tandon" title="Lalji Tandon">Lalji Tandon</a> was able to retain the <a href="/wiki/Lucknow_(Lok_Sabha_constituency)" class="mw-redirect" title="Lucknow (Lok Sabha constituency)">Lucknow</a> seat in that election even though the NDA suffered electoral reverses all over the country. It was speculated that Vajpayee's non-partisan appeal contributed to Lalji's success in Lucknow in contrast to that BJP's poor performance elsewhere in <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
