<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span></h2>
<p>Vajpayee remained a bachelor for his entire life.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> He adopted and raised Namita Bhattacharya as his own child, the daughter of longtime friend <a href="/wiki/Rajkumari_Kaul" title="Rajkumari Kaul">Rajkumari Kaul</a> and her husband B. N. Kaul. His adopted family lived with him.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unlike purist <a href="/wiki/Brahmin" title="Brahmin">Brahmins</a> who shun meat and alcohol, Vajpayee was known to be fond of <a href="/wiki/Whisky" title="Whisky">whisky</a> and meat.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> He was a noted poet, writing in Hindi. His <a href="#Published_works">published works</a> include <i>Kaidi Kaviraj Ki Kundalian</i>, a collection of poems written during the 1975–1977 emergency, and <i>Amar aag hai</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> With regard to his poetry he wrote, "My poetry is a declaration of war, not an <a href="/wiki/Exordium_(rhetoric)" class="mw-redirect" title="Exordium (rhetoric)">exordium</a> to defeat. It is not the defeated soldier's drumbeat of despair, but the fighting warrior's will to win. It is not the despirited voice of dejection but the stirring shout of victory."<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg/220px-Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg/330px-Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/28/Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg/440px-Atal_Bihari_Vajpayees_Funeral_Procession.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a><figcaption>Vajpayee's funeral procession moving to <a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Smriti Sthal near Raj Ghat</a> for last rites</figcaption></figure>
<p>Vajpayee had a stroke in 2009 which impaired his speech.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> His health had been a major source of concern; reports said he was reliant on a wheelchair and failed to recognise people. He also had <a href="/wiki/Dementia" title="Dementia">dementia</a> and long-term <a href="/wiki/Diabetes_mellitus" class="mw-redirect" title="Diabetes mellitus">diabetes</a>. For many years, he had not attended any public engagements and rarely ventured out of the house, except for checkups at the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Institutes_of_Medical_Sciences" title="All India Institutes of Medical Sciences">All India Institutes of Medical Sciences</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_143-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 11 June 2018, Vajpayee was admitted to AIIMS in critical condition following a kidney infection.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> He was officially declared dead there at 5:05&#160;pm <a href="/wiki/Indian_Standard_Time" title="Indian Standard Time">IST</a> on 16 August 2018 at the age of 93.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-TheHinduDeath_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TheHinduDeath-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> Some sources claim that he had died on the previous day.<sup id="cite_ref-ndtvDeath_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ndtvDeath-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-PuneMirrordeath_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PuneMirrordeath-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> On the morning of 17 August, Vajpayee's body, draped with the <a href="/wiki/Indian_flag" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian flag">Indian flag</a>, was taken to the Bharatiya Janata Party headquarters where party workers paid their tributes until 1&#160;pm. Later that afternoon at 4&#160;pm, Vajpayee was cremated with full state honours at Rashtriya Smriti Sthal near <a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Raj Ghat</a>, and his pyre was lit by his foster daughter Namita Kaul Bhattacharya.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Thousands of people and many dignitaries attended his funeral procession, including Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a> and President <a href="/wiki/Ram_Nath_Kovind" title="Ram Nath Kovind">Ram Nath Kovind</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> On 19 August, his ashes were immersed in <a href="/wiki/Ganges" title="Ganges">Ganga river</a> at <a href="/wiki/Haridwar" title="Haridwar">Haridwar</a> by Kaul.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Reactions_and_tributes">Reactions and tributes</span></h3>
<p>India reacted to Vajpayee's death with grief and thousands of tributes poured in through social media platforms. Thousands of people paid their respects during his funeral procession.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> A seven-day state mourning was announced by the central government throughout India. The national flag flew half-mast during this period.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Afghanistan: Former Afghan President <a href="/wiki/Hamid_Karzai" title="Hamid Karzai">Hamid Karzai</a> was among several foreign dignitaries present at former prime minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee's funeral in New Delhi. He recalled that the departed leader was "the first to offer us civilian planes, <a href="/wiki/Airbus" title="Airbus">Airbuses</a> at the time we were starting out".<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Bangladesh: Bangladesh Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Sheikh_Hasina" title="Sheikh Hasina">Sheikh Hasina</a> expressed "deep shock" at the demise of former Indian Prime Minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee and said it is a day of great sadness for the people of Bangladesh. Paying tribute to Vajpayee, Hasina termed him as "one of the most famous sons of India" and a highly respected person in Bangladesh.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Bhutan: Bhutan king <a href="/wiki/Jigme_Khesar_Namgyel_Wangchuck" title="Jigme Khesar Namgyel Wangchuck">Jigme Khesar Namgyel Wangchuck</a> attended the funeral ceremony in New Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>China: In a statement, the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_of_the_People%27s_Republic_of_China" title="Ministry of Foreign Affairs of the People&#39;s Republic of China">ministry of foreign affairs</a> said the Indian leader was an "outstanding Indian statesman and had made outstanding contributions to the development of Sino-Indian relations"."China expresses its deep condolences on his death and sincere condolences to the Indian government and people and the relatives of Mr Vajpayee. Premier <a href="/wiki/Li_Keqiang" title="Li Keqiang">Li Keqiang</a> has sent a condolence message to the leaders of India," the statement said.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Israel: <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Israel" title="Prime Minister of Israel">Prime Minister of Israel</a> <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu" title="Benjamin Netanyahu">Benjamin Netanyahu</a> conveyed his condolences calling Vajpayee "a true friend of Israel". <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Ministry_of_Israel" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign Ministry of Israel">Foreign Ministry of Israel</a> also extended its condolences on the passing of Vajpayee and in a statement described him as "a genuine friend of Israel".<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Japan: Remembering Vajpayee's visit to Japan in 2001, the Japanese Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Shinz%C5%8D_Abe" class="mw-redirect" title="Shinzō Abe">Shinzō Abe</a> said, "On behalf of the Government and people of Japan, I would like to convey my sincerest condolences to the Government and people of India and the bereaved family. His Excellency Vajpayee visited Japan in 2001 as the then-Prime Minister and made significant contributions to the friendship between our two countries as a good friend of Japan. It is him who established the cornerstone of Japan-India relations today". Terming Vajpayee as an eminent leader of India, Abe added, "I pray from the bottom of my heart that his soul may rest in peace".<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Mauritius: On 17 August, the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Mauritius" title="Government of Mauritius">government of Mauritius</a> announced that both Mauritian and Indian flags would fly at half mast in the honour of Vajpayee.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> During the <a href="/wiki/World_Hindi_Conference" title="World Hindi Conference">World Hindi Conference</a> in Mauritius, PM <a href="/wiki/Pravind_Jugnauth" title="Pravind Jugnauth">Pravind Jugnauth</a> announced that the cyber tower towards which Vajpayee contributed to be set up in Mauritius would be henceforth named as Atal Bihari Vajpayee tower.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Pakistan: Pakistan's interim Minister for <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Law_and_Justice_(Pakistan)" title="Ministry of Law and Justice (Pakistan)">Law and Information</a> Syed Ali Zafar met External Affairs Minister <a href="/wiki/Sushma_Swaraj" title="Sushma Swaraj">Sushma Swaraj</a> and extended Pakistan's condolence on the death of former prime minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee. Zafar was among the foreign dignitaries who attended Vajpayee's funeral in New Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Former Pakistani president <a href="/wiki/Pervez_Musharraf" title="Pervez Musharraf">Pervez Musharraf</a> mourned the demise of former prime minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee, calling him a great man. He said that Vajpayee's demise was a great loss for both India as well as Pakistan.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Russia: Russian President <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> sent a message of condolences to President Ram Nath Kovind and Prime Minister Narendra Modi on the demise of Vajpayee. Putin termed the former prime minister as "outstanding statesman". "Atal Bihari Vajpayee rightly commanded great respect around the world. He will be remembered as a politician who made a major personal contribution to the friendly relations and privileged strategic partnership between our countries. The President of Russia conveyed words of sincere sympathy and support to the family of the deceased, the Government and the people of India", the message read.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Sri Lanka: Various Sri Lankan leaders paid rich tribute to the three-time PM, hailing him as a "friend of Sri Lanka". In a <a href="/wiki/Twitter" title="Twitter">tweet</a> President <a href="/wiki/Maithripala_Sirisena" title="Maithripala Sirisena">Maithripala Sirisena</a> said: "Today, we have lost a great humanist and a true friend of Sri Lanka. Former Prime Minister of India Atal Bihari Vajpayee was a visionary leader and an ardent defender of democracy. My condolences to his family and millions of his admirers around the world". <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_(Sri_Lanka)" title="Leader of the Opposition (Sri Lanka)">Leader of Opposition</a> <a href="/wiki/R._Sampanthan" title="R. Sampanthan">R. Sampanthan</a> said that India has lost one of its "most regarded intellectual[s] and [statesmen]". "He served the great country of India with humility and honesty, and he was much loved and respected by millions of people across the world. Former three-time Prime Minister Vajpayee is also an exceptional orator and a leader with a great sense of humour, his speeches within the Indian parliament and outside will always be remembered", he said in a statement, extending his condolences on behalf of the Tamil people of Sri Lanka.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>United States: U.S. Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Michael_Pompeo" class="mw-redirect" title="Michael Pompeo">Michael Pompeo</a> said Vajpayee recognised early on that the US-India partnership would contribute to the world's economic prosperity and security and the two democracies would continue to benefit from his vision. "On behalf of the people of the United States of America, I extend my heartfelt condolences to the people of India on the recent passing of former prime minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee", Pompeo said in a statement yesterday. He recalled Vajpayee's address to the Congress in 2000, when he had famously characterised US-India ties as a "natural partnership of shared endeavours". "Today, our two countries and our bilateral relationship continue to benefit from Prime Minister Vajpayee vision, which helped promote expanded cooperation", Pompeo said. He said the American people stand with the people of India "as we mourn Prime Minister Vajpayee's passing".<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span id="Honours.2C_awards_and_international_recognition"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Honours,_awards_and_international_recognition">Honours, awards and international recognition</span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Awards">Awards</span></h3>
<ul><li>1993, <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_Letters" title="Doctor of Letters">D. Lit.</a> from <a href="/wiki/Chhatrapati_Shahu_Ji_Maharaj_University" title="Chhatrapati Shahu Ji Maharaj University">Kanpur University</a><sup id="cite_ref-:0_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1994, Lokmanya Tilak Award<sup id="cite_ref-:0_2-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1994, <a href="/wiki/Outstanding_Parliamentarian_Award" title="Outstanding Parliamentarian Award">Outstanding Parliamentarian Award</a><sup id="cite_ref-:0_2-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>1994, Bharat Ratna Pandit Govind Ballabh Pant Award<sup id="cite_ref-:0_2-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>

<tr>
<td><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_(Maroc).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_%28Maroc%29.svg/100px-Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_%28Maroc%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="28" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_%28Maroc%29.svg/150px-Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_%28Maroc%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_%28Maroc%29.svg/200px-Ordre_de_l%27Ouissam_Alaouite_Chevalier_ribbon_%28Maroc%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a></span>
</td>
<td><b><a href="/wiki/Order_of_Ouissam_Alaouite" title="Order of Ouissam Alaouite">Order of Ouissam Alaouite</a></b>
</td>
<td><span class="flagicon"><span class="mw-image-border" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Flag_of_Morocco.svg/23px-Flag_of_Morocco.svg.png" decoding="async" width="23" height="15" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Flag_of_Morocco.svg/35px-Flag_of_Morocco.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Flag_of_Morocco.svg/45px-Flag_of_Morocco.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="600" /></span></span>&#160;</span><a href="/wiki/Morocco" title="Morocco">Morocco</a>
</td>
<td>13 February 1999
</td>
<td><i>Grand Cordon</i>, the second-highest civilian honour of Morocco.
</td>
<td><sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg/100px-Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg.png" decoding="async" width="100" height="28" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg/150px-Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg/200px-Bharat_Ratna_Ribbon.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="218" data-file-height="60" /></a></span>

<td><span class="flagicon"><span class="mw-image-border" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/41/Flag_of_India.svg/23px-Flag_of_India.svg.png" decoding="async" width="23" height="15" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/41/Flag_of_India.svg/35px-Flag_of_India.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/4/41/Flag_of_India.svg/45px-Flag_of_India.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="600" /></span></span>&#160;</span><a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>

<td>27 March 2015
</td>
<td>The highest civilian honour of India.

<td>The second-highest honour of Bangladesh awarded to foreign dignitaries.
</td>
<td><sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_achievements">Other achievements</span></h3>
<ul><li>In 2012, Vajpayee was ranked number 9 in <i><a href="/wiki/Outlook_(Indian_magazine)" title="Outlook (Indian magazine)">Outlook</a></i> magazine's poll of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Greatest_Indian" title="The Greatest Indian">The Greatest Indian</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In August 2018, Naya Raipur was renamed as <a href="/wiki/Atal_Nagar" class="mw-redirect" title="Atal Nagar">Atal Nagar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-htorgNews_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-htorgNews-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In October 2018, four Himalayan peaks near Gangotri glacier named after his name.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Published_works">Published works</span></h2>
<p>Vajpayee authored several works of both <a href="/wiki/Hindi_poetry" class="mw-redirect" title="Hindi poetry">Hindi poetry</a> and <a href="/wiki/Prose" title="Prose">prose</a>. Some of his major publications are listed below. In addition to these, various collections were made of his speeches, articles, and slogans.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_181-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2000_182-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2000-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1977_183-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1977-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prose">Prose</span></h3>
<ul><li><i>National Integration</i> (1961)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1961_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1961-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>New Dimensions of India's Foreign Policy</i> (1979)<sup id="cite_ref-:4_181-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Gathbandhan Ki Rajniti</i><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2004_185-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2004-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Kuchh Lekh, Kuchh Bhashan</i> (1996)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1996_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1996-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Bindu-Bindu Vichar</i> (1997)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1997_187-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1997-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Decisive Days</i> (1999)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1999a_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1999a-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Sankalpakal</i> (1999)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1999b_189-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1999b-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Vichar-Bindu</i> (Hindi Edition, 2000)<sup id="cite_ref-:4_181-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>India's Perspectives on ASEAN and the Asia-Pacific Region</i> (2003)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2002_190-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2002-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Na Dainyam Na Palayanam</i><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1998_191-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1998-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Nayi Chunauti&#160;: Naya Avasar</i><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2011_192-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2011-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Poetry">Poetry</span></h3>
<ul><li><i>Qaidi Kaviraj Ki Kundaliyan</i><sup id="cite_ref-:4_181-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Amar Aag Hai</i> (1994)<sup id="cite_ref-:4_181-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Meri Ikyavan Kavitaen</i> (1995)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1995_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1995-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> Some of these poems were set to music by <a href="/wiki/Jagjit_Singh" title="Jagjit Singh">Jagjit Singh</a> for his album <i>Samvedna</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-singh_music_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-singh_music-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Kya Khoya Kya Paya: Atal Bihari Vajapeyi, Vyaktitva Aur Kavitaen</i> (1999)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1999c_195-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee1999c-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Values, Vision &amp; Verses of Vajpayee: India's Man of Destiny</i> (2001)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2001a_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2001a-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Twenty-One Poems</i> (2003)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2001b_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2001b-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Chuni Hui Kavitaen</i> (2012)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2012_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2012-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>An English translation of a selection of some of Vajpayee's Hindi poetry was published in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2013_199-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVajpayee2013-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Category:Memorials_to_Atal_Bihari_Vajpayee" title="Category:Memorials to Atal Bihari Vajpayee">Category:Memorials to Atal Bihari Vajpayee</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg/220px-Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="89" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg/330px-Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg/440px-Prime_Minister_Modi_at_Sadaiv_Atal.jpg 2x" data-file-width="770" data-file-height="311" /></a><figcaption>Prime Minister Modi at Vajpayee's memorial, <a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Sadaiv Atal</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The administration of <a href="/wiki/Narendra_Modi" title="Narendra Modi">Narendra Modi</a> declared in 2014 that Vajpayee's birthday, 25&#160;December, would be marked as <a href="/wiki/Good_Governance_Day" title="Good Governance Day">Good Governance Day</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup>
The world's longest tunnel, <a href="/wiki/Atal_Tunnel" title="Atal Tunnel">Atal Tunnel</a> at <a href="/wiki/Rohtang" class="mw-redirect" title="Rohtang">Rohtang</a>, Himachal Pradesh, on the Leh-Manali Highway was named after Atal Bihari Vajpayee.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup>
The third longest cable-stayed bridge in India over the <a href="/wiki/Mandovi_River" title="Mandovi River">Mandovi River</a>, <a href="/wiki/Atal_Setu_(Goa)" class="mw-redirect" title="Atal Setu (Goa)">Atal Setu</a> was named in his memory.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Chhattisgarh" title="Government of Chhattisgarh">Government of Chhattisgarh</a> changed the name of <a href="/wiki/Naya_Raipur" title="Naya Raipur">Naya Raipur</a> to <a href="/wiki/Atal_Nagar" class="mw-redirect" title="Atal Nagar">Atal Nagar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_popular_culture">In popular culture</span></h2>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Films_Division_of_India" title="Films Division of India">Films Division of India</a> has produced the short documentary films <i>Pride of India Atal Bihari Vajpayee</i> (1998) and <i>Know Your Prime Minister Atal Behari Vajpayee</i> (2003), both directed by Girish Vaidya, which explore different facets of his personality.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> Vajpayee also appears in a cameo in the 1977 Indian <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a>-language film <i><a href="/wiki/Chala_Murari_Hero_Banne" title="Chala Murari Hero Banne">Chala Murari Hero Banne</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Asrani" title="Asrani">Asrani</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2019, Shiva Sharma and Zeeshan Ahmad, owners of Amaash Films, acquired the official rights of the book <i>The Untold Vajpayee written</i> by Ullekh N P, to make a biopic based on Vajpayee's life from his childhood, college life and finally turning into a politician.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Aap_Ki_Adalat" title="Aap Ki Adalat">Aap Ki Adalat</a></i>, an Indian talk show which airs on <a href="/wiki/India_TV" title="India TV">India TV</a>, featured an interview with Vajpayee just before the 1999 elections.<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Pradhanmantri" title="Pradhanmantri">Pradhanmantri</a></i> (<abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Prime Minister&#39;), a 2013 Indian documentary television series which aired on <a href="/wiki/ABP_News" title="ABP News">ABP News</a> and covers the various policies and political tenures of Indian PMs, includes the tenureship of Vajpayee in the episodes "Atal Bihari Vajpayee's 13 days government and India during 1996–98", "Pokhran-II and Kargil War", and "2002 Gujarat Riots and Fall of Vajpayee Government".<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
